\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \rho_{n}&\to\rho\qquad\text{in }C\bigl([0,T];L^{p}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\bigr),\qquad\forall p\in[1,\infty),\\
\sqrt{\rho_{n}}\,u_{n}&\to\sqrt{\rho}\,u\qquad\text{in }L^{1}\bigl((0,T);L^{2}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\bigr),\\
\rho_{n}u_{n,j}&\to\rho u_{j}\qquad\text{in }L^{1}\bigl((0,T);L^{2}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\bigr).\end{aligned}

In particular, combining (
4.129
) with the strong convergence of
\rho_{n}
, we infer that

(4.131)

\displaystyle\rho_{n}w_{n}\rightharpoonup^{*}\rho w\qquad\text{in }L^{\infty}\bigl((0,T);L^{2}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\bigr).

Since
w_{n}
solves (
4.118
), for every divergence-free

\varphi\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
and every
0\leq s\leq t\leq T
we have

(4.132)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho_{n}(t)\,w_{n}(t)\cdot\varphi-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho_{n}(s)\,w_{n}(s)\cdot\varphi\\
&\qquad=-\int_{s}^{t}\!\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(\rho_{n}u_{n}\otimes w_{n}):\nabla\varphi+\int_{s}^{t}\!\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\nabla w_{n}:\nabla\varphi+\int_{s}^{t}\!\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho_{n}u_{n,j}\cdot\varphi.\end{aligned}

Using (
4.126
) and (
4.130
), we deduce that there exists a constant
C>0
, independent of
n
,
s
, and
t
, such that

(4.133)

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho_{n}(t)\,w_{n}(t)\cdot\varphi-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho_{n}(s)\,w_{n}(s)\cdot\varphi\right|\leq C|t-s|\,\|\varphi\|_{W^{1,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}.

Hence, for every fixed
\varphi\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
, the family of functions

(4.134)

\displaystyle t\mapsto\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho_{n}(t)\,w_{n}(t)\cdot\varphi

is equibounded and equicontinuous on
[0,T]
. Therefore, up to a subsequence, it converges uniformly on
[0,T]
. In view of (
4.131
), the limit is necessarily

(4.135)

\displaystyle t\mapsto\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho(t)\,w(t)\cdot\varphi.

Since
\rho_{n}w_{n}
is uniformly bounded in
L^{\infty}\bigl((0,T);L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\bigr)
, by density we conclude that for every
t\in[0,T]
,

(4.136)

\displaystyle\rho_{n}\,w_{n}(t)\rightharpoonup\rho\,w(t)\qquad\text{in }L_{\sigma}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2}).

Step 3.
Conclusion.

Since
u_{n,j}
solves

(4.137)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\partial_{t}(\rho_{n}u_{n,j})+\dive(\rho_{n}u_{n}\otimes u_{n,j})+\nabla P_{n,j}=\Delta u_{n,j},\\
\dive u_{n,j}=0,\\
u_{n,j}(0)=u_{0,j},\end{cases}

the system (
4.118
) is dual to (
4.137
), and therefore

(4.138)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho_{n}u_{n,j}\cdot u_{n,j}\,\,\mathrm{d}x\,\,\mathrm{d}t=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho_{n}(0,x)\,w_{n}(0,x)\cdot u_{0,j}(x)\,\,\mathrm{d}x.

Thanks to (
4.136
) and the convergence (
4.85
), we may pass to the limit as
n\to\infty
and obtain (
4.116
).

We may also pass to the limit in (
4.132
), using again (
4.136
) together with the convergences in (
4.126
) and (
4.130
). Then, taking
\varphi=a\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
(this can be justified as in
Lemma

3.11
), we obtain

(4.139)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho(t)\,w(t)\cdot a\,\,\mathrm{d}x-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho(s)\,w(s)\cdot a\,\,\mathrm{d}x=\int_{s}^{t}\!\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho u_{j}\cdot a\,\,\mathrm{d}x\,\,\mathrm{d}\tau.

By
Lemma

3.11
,

(4.140)

\displaystyle\int_{s}^{t}\!\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho u_{j}\cdot a\,\,\mathrm{d}x\,\,\mathrm{d}\tau=a\cdot\int_{s}^{t}\!\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho_{0}u_{0,j}\,\,\mathrm{d}x\,\,\mathrm{d}\tau=(t-s)\,a\cdot\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho_{0}u_{0,j}\,\,\mathrm{d}x.

Hence,

(4.141)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho(t)\,w(t)\,\,\mathrm{d}x-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho(s)\,w(s)\,\,\mathrm{d}x=(t-s)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho_{0}u_{0,j}\,\,\mathrm{d}x.

Setting
t=T
and using
w(T)=0
, we obtain (
4.117
).
∎

5.
Existence and Uniqueness for Besov data

In this section, we prove a uniqueness result under the following assumptions on the initial data:

(5.1)

\displaystyle\rho_{0}=\mathbf{1}_{D},\qquad D\text{ bounded Lipschitz domain,}\qquad u_{0}\in\dot{B}^{0}_{2,1}(\mathbb{R}^{2}).

We also derive key
L^{1}
-in-time estimates.

Since
\dot{B}^{0}_{2,1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\subset L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
, we can use the decomposition (
4.59
). Namely, for every
\eta\in(0,1)
there exists a sequence
(u_{0,j})_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}
in

\dot{H}^{\eta}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\cap\dot{H}^{-\eta}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
such that

(5.2)

\displaystyle u_{0}=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}u_{0,j},\qquad c_{j}:=2^{-j/2}\left\lVert u_{0,j}\right\rVert_{\dot{H}^{\eta}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+2^{j/2}\left\lVert u_{0,j}\right\rVert_{\dot{H}^{-\eta}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\in\ell^{2}(\mathbb{Z}).

For the rest of the section, let
(\rho,u)
be the solution of (
1.2
) with initial data
(\rho_{0},u_{0})
constructed in
Proposition

4.1
. For every
j\in\mathbb{Z}
, let
u_{j}
be the solution of (
4.60
) constructed in
Lemma

4.5
with initial datum
u_{0,j}
.

In Sections 5.1 and 5.2 we exploit the additional information
u_{0,j}\in\dot{H}^{-\eta}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
to obtain improved decay estimates for
u_{j}
, in contrast with
Lemma

4.5
, where only positive regularity is used. We emphasize that these arguments only rely on the assumption
u_{0}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
.

Finally, in Section 5.3 we show that the additional structure provided by
u_{0}\in\dot{B}^{0}_{2,1}
yields further regularity properties for
(\rho,u)
, which are sufficient to establish uniqueness.

We restrict to
\eta\in(0,\tfrac{1}{2})
. This is natural since, for Lipschitz domains, the characteristic function
\rho_{0}=\mathbf{1}_{D}
belongs to the multiplier space
\mathcal{M}(\dot{H}^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{2}))
for all
s\in(-\tfrac{1}{2},\tfrac{1}{2})
(
[
32
, Proposition 5.3]
). In particular, for every
\eta\in(0,\tfrac{1}{2})
there exists a constant
C>0
such that, for every
\phi\in\dot{H}^{\pm\eta}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
,

(5.3)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\rho_{0}\phi\right\rVert_{\dot{H}^{-\eta}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq C\left\lVert\phi\right\rVert_{\dot{H}^{-\eta}(\mathbb{R}^{2})},\qquad\left\lVert\rho_{0}\phi\right\rVert_{\dot{H}^{\eta}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq C\left\lVert\phi\right\rVert_{\dot{H}^{\eta}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}.

5.1.
Galilean transform and estimates for
\dot{H}^{-\eta}
data

Here and in the following, let

(5.4)

\displaystyle M_{j}=\frac{1}{|D|}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho_{0}u_{0,j}\,\,\mathrm{d}x,\qquad j\in\mathbb{Z},

and define, for every
j\in\mathbb{Z}
, every
t\geq 0
, and every
x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
,

(5.5)

\displaystyle\rho_{M_{j}}(t,x)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\rho(t,x+M_{j}t),

(5.6)

\displaystyle u_{M_{j}}(t,x)

\displaystyle\coloneqq u(t,x+M_{j}t)-M_{j},

(5.7)

\displaystyle u_{j,M_{j}}(t,x)

\displaystyle\coloneqq u_{j}(t,x+M_{j}t)-M_{j}.

This transform, called the Galilean transform, satisfies the following two basic but crucial properties.

Lemma 5.1
.

Let
(\rho_{M_{j}},u_{M_{j}})
and
u_{j,M_{j}}
be as above. Then, for every
t\geq 0
,

(5.8)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho_{M_{j}}(t,x)\,u_{j,M_{j}}(t,x)\,\,\mathrm{d}x=0.

Moreover, for every
0\leq s<t
,

(5.9)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\|\sqrt{\rho_{M_{j}}(t)}\,u_{j,M_{j}}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\int_{s}^{t}\|\nabla u_{j}(\tau)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\,\,\mathrm{d}\tau=\frac{1}{2}\|\sqrt{\rho_{M_{j}}(s)}\,u_{j,M_{j}}(s)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}.

Proof.

Since
u_{j}
is an immediately strong solution by
Lemma

4.5
, its momentum is conserved in time by
Lemma

3.11
. Therefore,

(5.10)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho(t,x)\,u_{j}(t,x)\,\,\mathrm{d}x=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho_{0}(x)\,u_{0,j}(x)\,\,\mathrm{d}x=|D|\,M_{j}.

Since

(5.11)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho(t,x)\,\,\mathrm{d}x=|D|,

identity (
5.8
) follows directly from the definition of the transform.

This proves (
5.8
). Recall now that
u_{j}
satisfies the energy equality, namely, for every
0\leq s<t
,

(5.12)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho(t)|u_{j}(t)|^{2}\,\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{s}^{t}\|\nabla u_{j}(\tau)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\,\,\mathrm{d}\tau=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho(s)|u_{j}(s)|^{2}\,\,\mathrm{d}x.

If we show that, for every
t\geq 0
,

(5.13)

\displaystyle\|\sqrt{\rho_{M_{j}}(t)}\,u_{j,M_{j}}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho(t,x)\,|u_{j}(t,x)|^{2}\,\,\mathrm{d}x-|D|\,|M_{j}|^{2},

then inserting (
5.13
), evaluated at times
t
and
s
, into (
5.12
) yields (
5.9
).

To prove (
5.13
), we expand the square and use (
5.10
) together with (
5.11
):

(5.14)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho_{M_{j}}(t,x)|u_{j,M_{j}}(t,x)|^{2}\,\,\mathrm{d}x&=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho(t,x+M_{j}t)\,|u_{j}(t,x+M_{j}t)-M_{j}|^{2}\,\,\mathrm{d}x\\
&=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho(t,x+M_{j}t)\,|u_{j}(t,x+M_{j}t)|^{2}\,\,\mathrm{d}x\\
&\quad-2M_{j}\cdot\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho(t,x+M_{j}t)\,u_{j}(t,x+M_{j}t)\,\,\mathrm{d}x\\
&\quad+|M_{j}|^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho(t,x+M_{j}t)\,\,\mathrm{d}x\\
&=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho(t,x)\,|u_{j}(t,x)|^{2}\,\,\mathrm{d}x-|D|\,|M_{j}|^{2}.\end{aligned}

This concludes the proof.
∎

Since
u_{j,M_{j}}
has zero
\rho_{M_{j}}
-momentum, we can eliminate the constant component and recover a Poincaré-type control. This yields improved decay estimates, as shown in the following lemma.

Lemma 5.2
.

There exists a constant
C>0
, depending only on
D
and
\|u_{0}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}
, such that for every
t>0
,

(5.15)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\|\sqrt{\rho_{M_{j}}(s)}\,u_{j,M_{j}}(s)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C\|u_{0,j}\|_{\dot{H}^{-\eta}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}\,t^{1-\eta},

and

(5.16)

\displaystyle\|\sqrt{\rho_{M_{j}}(t)}\,u_{j,M_{j}}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}\leq C\|u_{0,j}\|_{\dot{H}^{-\eta}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}\,t^{-\eta}.

Proof.

Let
w_{j}^{t}
be the solution to (
4.114
) on
[0,t]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
. By
Lemma

4.8
and the definition of
M_{j}
, we have

(5.17)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho_{0}(x)\,w_{j}^{t}(0,x)\,\,\mathrm{d}x=-t\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho_{0}(x)\,u_{0,j}(x)\,\,\mathrm{d}x=-t|D|\,M_{j}.

We introduce

(5.18)

\displaystyle\bar{w}_{0}(x):=w_{j}^{t}(0,x)+tM_{j},

which has zero mean on
D
thanks to (
5.17
) and the fact that
\rho_{0}=\mathbf{1}_{D}
. By duality,

(5.19)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\|\sqrt{\rho}(s)\,u_{j}(s)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}\,\,\mathrm{d}s=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho_{0}(x)\,u_{0,j}(x)\cdot w_{j}^{t}(0,x)\,\,\mathrm{d}x.

Adding
-t|D||M_{j}|^{2}
to both sides and using (
5.13
), we can rewrite (
5.19
) as

(5.20)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\|\sqrt{\rho_{M_{j}}}(s)\,u_{j,M_{j}}(s)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}\,\,\mathrm{d}s=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho_{0}(x)\,u_{0,j}(x)\cdot\bar{w}_{0}(x)\,\,\mathrm{d}x\leq\|u_{0,j}\|_{\dot{H}^{-\eta}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\|\rho_{0}\bar{w}_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{\eta}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}.

To prove (
5.15
), it suffices to show

(5.21)

\displaystyle\|\rho_{0}\bar{w}_{0}\|^{2}_{\dot{H}^{\eta}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq Ct^{1-\eta}\int_{0}^{t}\|\sqrt{\rho_{M_{j}}}(s)\,u_{j,M_{j}}(s)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}\,\,\mathrm{d}s.

Since
D
is a Lipschitz domain and
\bar{w}_{0}
has zero average on
D
, we can apply the Poincaré inequality on
D
and arguing as in (
1.77
) obtain

(5.22)

\displaystyle\|\rho_{0}\bar{w}_{0}\|^{2}_{\dot{H}^{\eta}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\bar{w}_{0}\|_{L^{2}(D)}^{2-2\eta}\,\|\bar{w}_{0}\|_{H^{1}(D)}^{2\eta}\lesssim\|\bar{w}_{0}\|_{L^{2}(D)}^{2-2\eta}\left(\|\bar{w}_{0}\|_{L^{2}(D)}^{2}+\|\nabla\bar{w}_{0}\|_{L^{2}(D)}^{2}\right)^{\eta}

(5.23)

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\bar{w}_{0}\|_{L^{2}(D)}^{2-2\eta}\|\nabla\bar{w}_{0}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2\eta}.

The implicit constants depend only on
D
.

By
Lemma

4.8
, there exists
C>0
such that

(5.24)

\displaystyle t^{-1}\|\sqrt{\rho_{0}}w^{t}_{j}(0)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\|\nabla w^{t}_{j}(0)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq\int_{0}^{t}\|\sqrt{\rho}(s)\,u_{j}(s)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s.

Moreover, a direct computation shows that

(5.25)

\displaystyle\|\sqrt{\rho_{0}}\,\bar{w}_{0}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}=\|\sqrt{\rho_{0}}\,w_{j}^{t}(0)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}-t^{2}|D|\,|M_{j}|^{2}.

Using (
5.13
) and (
5.25
), we deduce

(5.26)

\displaystyle t^{-1}\|\sqrt{\rho_{0}}\bar{w}_{0}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\|\nabla\bar{w}_{0}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq\int_{0}^{t}\|\sqrt{\rho_{M_{j}}(s)}\,u_{j,M_{j}}(s)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s.

Inserting (
5.26
) into (
5.22
) yields (
5.21
).

To prove (
5.16
), we observe that by (
5.9
) the quantity

(5.27)

\displaystyle\|\sqrt{\rho_{M_{j}}(t)}\,u_{j,M_{j}}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}

is non-increasing in time. Therefore,

(5.28)

\displaystyle\,t\,\|\sqrt{\rho_{M_{j}}(t)}\,u_{j,M_{j}}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}\leq\int_{0}^{t}\|\sqrt{\rho_{M_{j}}(s)}\,u_{j,M_{j}}(s)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s,

and inserting this into (
5.15
) gives (
5.16
).
∎

5.2.
L^{1}
-in-time estimates

Combining the shifted estimates (
4.65
) with the decay of the Galilean transform obtained in
Lemma

5.2
, we obtain decay estimates for the solution
u_{j}
.

Lemma 5.3
.

There exists a constant
C>0
, depending only on
D
and
\|u_{0}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}
, such that for every
s>0
and for almost every
t>0
,

(5.29)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \|\nabla u_{j}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}&\leq C\,t^{-1/\penalty 2-\eta/2}\|u_{0,j}\|_{\dot{H}^{-\eta}(\mathbb{R}^{2})},\\
\|\sqrt{\rho(t)}\,\dot{u}_{j}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}&\leq C\,t^{-1-\eta/2}\|u_{0,j}\|_{\dot{H}^{-\eta}(\mathbb{R}^{2})},\\
\|\nabla\dot{u}_{j}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}&\leq C\,t^{-3/\penalty 2-\eta/2}\|u_{0,j}\|_{\dot{H}^{-\eta}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}.\end{aligned}

Proof.

In the proof, every constant depending only on
D
and
\|u_{0}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}
is denoted by
C
.

We first prove the estimate for
\nabla u_{j}
. By (
4.65
), for every
0<s<T<\infty
,

(5.30)

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in(s,T)}(t-s)\|\nabla u_{j}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}\leq C\int_{s}^{T}\|\nabla u_{j}(\tau)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}\,\,\mathrm{d}\tau.

Then, by the energy equality (
5.9
) and the decay estimate (
5.16
), we get

(5.31)

\displaystyle\int_{s}^{T}\|\nabla u_{j}(\tau)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}\,\,\mathrm{d}\tau\leq\frac{1}{2}\|\sqrt{\rho_{M_{j}}(s)}\,u_{j,M_{j}}(s)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}\leq Cs^{-\eta}\|u_{0,j}\|_{\dot{H}^{-\eta}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}.

Combining (
5.30
) and (
5.31
), we infer that for every
0<s<T<\infty
,

(5.32)

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in(s,T)}(t-s)\|\nabla u_{j}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}\leq Cs^{-\eta}\|u_{0,j}\|_{\dot{H}^{-\eta}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}.

Since
T>0
is arbitrary, it follows that for every
s>0
and for almost every
t>s
,

(5.33)

\displaystyle(t-s)\|\nabla u_{j}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}\leq Cs^{-\eta}\|u_{0,j}\|_{\dot{H}^{-\eta}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}.

By setting
s=t/\penalty 2
we conclude the proof and taking square roots, we obtain the first estimate in (
5.29
). The other two estimates are proved in the same way, starting from the corresponding bounds in (
4.65
).
∎

Now we study a time-integrated version of
Lemma

5.3
. This combines the decay estimates obtained in (
5.29
) with the bounds provided by
Lemma

4.5
, in particular (
4.64
).

Lemma 5.4
.

There exists a constant
C>0
, depending only on
D
and
\|u_{0}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}
, such that the following estimates hold.

(i)

Let
\beta_{1}>0
be such that
\beta_{1}=2(\alpha_{1}+1)
. Then

(5.34)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{\alpha_{1}}\|\nabla u_{j}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{\beta_{1}}\,dt\leq C\Big(2^{-\frac{j}{2}\beta_{1}}\|u_{0,j}\|_{\dot{H}^{\eta}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{\beta_{1}}+2^{\frac{j}{2}\beta_{1}}\|u_{0,j}\|_{\dot{H}^{-\eta}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{\beta_{1}}\Big).

(ii)

Let
\beta_{2}>0
be such that
\beta_{2}=\alpha_{2}+1
. Then

(5.35)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{\alpha_{2}}\|\sqrt{\rho(t)}\,\dot{u}_{j}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{\beta_{2}}\,dt\leq C\Big(2^{-\frac{j}{2}\beta_{2}}\|u_{0,j}\|_{\dot{H}^{\eta}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{\beta_{2}}+2^{\frac{j}{2}\beta_{2}}\|u_{0,j}\|_{\dot{H}^{-\eta}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{\beta_{2}}\Big).

(iii)

Let
\beta_{3}>0
be such that
3\beta_{3}=2(\alpha_{3}+1)
. Then

(5.36)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{\alpha_{3}}\|\nabla\dot{u}_{j}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{\beta_{3}}\,dt\leq C\Big(2^{-\frac{j}{2}\beta_{3}}\|u_{0,j}\|_{\dot{H}^{\eta}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{\beta_{3}}+2^{\frac{j}{2}\beta_{3}}\|u_{0,j}\|_{\dot{H}^{-\eta}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{\beta_{3}}\Big).

Proof.

Throughout the proof, constants depending only on
D
and
\|u_{0}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}
are omitted and denoted by
\lesssim
.

We start with (i). By
Lemma

5.3
with
s=t/2
, we obtain, for almost every
t>0
,

(5.37)

\displaystyle\|\nabla u_{j}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\lesssim\|u_{0,j}\|_{\dot{H}^{-\eta}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\,t^{-1/2-\eta/2}.

On the other hand, by
Lemma

4.5
(see (
4.64
)), we have, for almost every
t>0
,

(5.38)

\displaystyle\|\nabla u_{j}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\lesssim\min\Big\{\|u_{0,j}\|_{\dot{H}^{\eta}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\,t^{-1/2+\eta/2},\,\|u_{0,j}\|_{\dot{H}^{-\eta}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\,t^{-1/2-\eta/2}\Big\}.

Let
A>0
. Splitting the integral at
A
and using (
5.38
), we obtain

(5.39)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{\alpha_{1}}\|\nabla u_{j}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{\beta_{1}}\,dt

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u_{0,j}\|_{\dot{H}^{\eta}}^{\beta_{1}}\int_{0}^{A}t^{\alpha_{1}+(-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\eta}{2})\beta_{1}}\,dt+\|u_{0,j}\|_{\dot{H}^{-\eta}}^{\beta_{1}}\int_{A}^{\infty}t^{\alpha_{1}+(-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\eta}{2})\beta_{1}}\,dt.

Using
\beta_{1}=2(\alpha_{1}+1)
, we compute

(5.40)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{\alpha_{1}}\|\nabla u_{j}(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{\beta_{1}}\,dt\lesssim\|u_{0,j}\|_{\dot{H}^{\eta}}^{\beta_{1}}A^{\frac{\eta}{2}\beta_{1}}+\|u_{0,j}\|_{\dot{H}^{-\eta}}^{\beta_{1}}A^{-\frac{\eta}{2}\beta_{1}}.

Choosing
A=2^{-j/\eta}
yields (i).

The proof of (ii) is identical. Indeed, using again
Lemma

4.5
and
Lemma

5.3
, we obtain, for almost every
t>0
,

(5.41)

\displaystyle\|\sqrt{\rho(t)}\,\dot{u}_{j}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\lesssim\min\Big\{\|u_{0,j}\|_{\dot{H}^{\eta}}\,t^{-1+\eta/2},\,\|u_{0,j}\|_{\dot{H}^{-\eta}}\,t^{-1-\eta/2}\Big\}.

Proceeding as above yields (ii). The proof of (iii) is analogous.
∎

5.3.
Lipschitz regularity of Besov solutions.

We now exploit the assumption
u_{0}\in\dot{B}^{0}_{2,1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
.
Since this regularity is more evenly distributed across frequencies, we may assume that the initial decomposition (
4.1
) satisfies the additional summability property

(5.42)

\displaystyle c_{j}:=2^{-j/2}\|u_{0,j}\|_{\dot{H}^{\eta}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+2^{j/2}\|\rho_{0}u_{0,j}\|_{\dot{H}^{-\eta}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\in\ell^{1}(\mathbb{Z}).

The fact that
c_{j}\in\ell^{1}(\mathbb{Z})
ensures that the solution constructed in
Proposition

4.6
is Lipschitz.
We also note that
u_{0}
belongs to the same weighted spaces considered in
[
15
]
.

Proposition 5.5
.

Let
(\rho,u)
be the solution constructed in
Proposition

4.1
, and assume in addition that
u_{0}\in\dot{B}^{0}_{2,1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
. Then there exists a constant
C>0
, depending only on
D
and
\|u_{0}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}
, such that

(5.43)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\|\sqrt{\rho}\dot{u}(t),\nabla^{2}u(t),\nabla P(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\,\,\mathrm{d}t+\int_{0}^{\infty}\sqrt{t}\,\|\nabla\dot{u}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\,\,\mathrm{d}t\leq C\|u_{0}\|_{\dot{B}^{0}_{2,1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}.

Moreover,

(5.44)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{-1/2}\|\nabla u(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\|\nabla u(t)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\,\,\mathrm{d}t+\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{3/4}\|\nabla\dot{u}(t)\|_{L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\,\,\mathrm{d}t\leq C\|u_{0}\|_{\dot{B}^{0}_{2,1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}.

Furthermore,

(5.45)

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to 0}\sqrt{t}\,\|\nabla u(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}=0.

Proof.

Similarly as before, throughout the proof we omit constants depending only on
D
and
\|u_{0}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}
, and denote them by
\lesssim
.

For every
j\in\mathbb{Z}
, let
u_{j}
be the solution to the linearized system (
4.60
)
with initial data
u_{0,j}
constructed in
Lemma

4.5
. For every
J\in\mathbb{N}
, define

(5.46)

\displaystyle u_{J}:=\sum_{|j|\leq J}u_{j}.

We start with (
5.43
). By
Lemma

5.4
(ii) with
\alpha_{2}=0
and
\beta_{2}=1
, we obtain

(5.47)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\|\rho\dot{u}_{j}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\,\,\mathrm{d}t\lesssim c_{j}.

Summing over
|j|\leq J
and using that
c_{j}\in\ell^{1}(\mathbb{Z})
, we deduce the uniform bound

(5.48)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\|\rho\dot{u}_{J}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\,\,\mathrm{d}t\leq\sum_{|j|\leq J}\int_{0}^{\infty}\|\rho\dot{u}_{j}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\,\,\mathrm{d}t\lesssim\|u_{0}\|_{\dot{B}^{0}_{2,1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}.

By
Proposition

4.6
(see (
4.99
)), we have

(5.49)

\displaystyle\rho\dot{u}_{J}\to\rho\dot{u}\qquad\text{in }\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\bigl((0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{2}\bigr).

Therefore, by lower semicontinuity (see
Remark

4.3
), we conclude

(5.50)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\|\rho\dot{u}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\,\,\mathrm{d}t\leq\liminf_{J\to\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}\|\rho\dot{u}_{J}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\,\,\mathrm{d}t\lesssim\|u_{0}\|_{\dot{B}^{0}_{2,1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}.

This proves the first estimate in (
5.43
). By linear Stokes, we immediately obtain that

(5.51)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\|\nabla^{2}u(t),\nabla P(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\,\,\mathrm{d}t\lesssim\int_{0}^{\infty}\|\sqrt{\rho}\dot{u}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\,\,\mathrm{d}t\leq C\|u_{0}\|_{\dot{B}^{0}_{2,1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}.

The second estimate in (
5.43
) follows in the same way by using
Lemma

5.4
(iii) with
\alpha_{3}=\tfrac{1}{2}
and
\beta_{3}=1
.

In order to prove the first estimate in (
5.44
), we use
Lemma

5.4
(i) with
\alpha_{1}=-1/2
and
\beta_{1}=1
and we obtain that, for every
j\in\mathbb{Z}
,

(5.52)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{-1/2}\left\lVert\nabla u_{j}(t)\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\,\,\mathrm{d}t\lesssim c_{j}.

By summing over all
j\in\mathbb{Z}
and the same arguments as above, we conclude the first bound in (
5.44
). To show the second bound in (
5.44
), we recall that by (
2.50
) (and Stokes estimates), for every
j\in\mathbb{Z}
and every
t>0
,

(5.53)

\displaystyle\|\nabla u_{j}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\lesssim\|\rho(t)\dot{u}_{j}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\|\nabla u_{j}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{1/2}\|\nabla\dot{u}_{j}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{1/2}.

We have already estimated the first term. For the second one, we use Hölder’s inequality with exponents
4/3
and
4
, together with
Lemma

5.4
(i) with
\alpha_{1}=-2/3
and
\beta_{1}=2/3
, and
Lemma

5.4
(iii) with
\alpha_{3}=2
and
\beta_{3}=2
, to obtain

(5.54)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}

\displaystyle\|\nabla u_{j}(s)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{1/2}\|\nabla\dot{u}_{j}(s)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{1/2}\,ds

(5.55)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}s^{-2/3}\|\nabla u_{j}(s)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2/3}\,ds\right)^{3/4}\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}s^{2}\|\nabla\dot{u}_{j}(s)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}\,ds\right)^{1/4}

(5.56)

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(2^{-j/3}\|u_{0,j}\|_{\dot{H}^{\eta}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2/3}+2^{j/3}\|u_{0,j}\|_{\dot{H}^{-\eta}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2/3}\right)^{3/4}\left(2^{-j}\|u_{0,j}\|_{\dot{H}^{\eta}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}+2^{j}\|u_{0,j}\|_{\dot{H}^{-\eta}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}\right)^{1/4}\lesssim c_{j}.

Since
(c_{j})_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\in\ell^{1}(\mathbb{Z})
, we conclude as before.

To complete the proof of (
5.44
), it remains to show that

(5.57)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\|t^{3/4}\nabla\dot{u}_{j}(t)\|_{L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\,\,\mathrm{d}t\lesssim c_{j}.

Fix
j\in\mathbb{Z}
. By Ladyzhenskaya’s inequality and Hölder’s inequality, we obtain

(5.58)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\|t^{3/4}\nabla\dot{u}_{j}(t)\|_{L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\,\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{3/4}\|\nabla\dot{u}_{j}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{1/2}\|\nabla^{2}\dot{u}_{j}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{1/2}\,\,\mathrm{d}t

(5.59)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}\|\nabla\dot{u}_{j}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2/3}\,\,\mathrm{d}t\right)^{3/4}\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{3}\|\nabla^{2}\dot{u}_{j}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}\,\,\mathrm{d}t\right)^{1/4}.

By
Lemma

4.5
, we have

(5.60)

\displaystyle\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{3}\|\nabla^{2}\dot{u}_{j}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}\,\,\mathrm{d}t\right)^{1/4}\lesssim\|u_{0,j}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{1/2}\lesssim\left(2^{-j/4}\|u_{0,j}\|_{\dot{H}^{\eta}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{1/2}+2^{j/4}\|u_{0,j}\|_{\dot{H}^{-\eta}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{1/2}\right),

while by
Lemma

5.4
(iii), applied with
\alpha_{3}=0
and
\beta_{3}=2/3
, we obtain

(5.61)

\displaystyle\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}\|\nabla\dot{u}_{j}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2/3}\,\,\mathrm{d}t\right)^{3/4}\lesssim\left(2^{-j/4}\|u_{0,j}\|_{\dot{H}^{\eta}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{1/2}+2^{j/4}\|u_{0,j}\|_{\dot{H}^{-\eta}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{1/2}\right).

Multiplying the above estimates yields (
5.57
). By (
5.38
), for
j\in\mathbb{Z}
, and almost every
t>0
,

(5.62)

\displaystyle\sqrt{t}\,\|\nabla u_{j}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\lesssim\min\left\{t^{\eta/2}\|u_{0,j}\|_{\dot{H}^{\eta}(\mathbb{R}^{2})},\;t^{-\eta/2}\|u_{0,j}\|_{\dot{H}^{-\eta}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\right\}.

Then, for every
j\in\mathbb{Z}
, we have

(5.63)

\displaystyle\sqrt{t}\,\|\nabla u_{j}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

\displaystyle\lesssim t^{\eta/2}\|u_{0,j}\|_{\dot{H}^{\eta}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq Ct^{\eta/2}2^{j/2}c_{j},

(5.64)

\displaystyle\sqrt{t}\,\|\nabla u_{j}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

\displaystyle\lesssim t^{-\eta/2}\|u_{0,j}\|_{\dot{H}^{-\eta}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq Ct^{-\eta/2}2^{-j/2}c_{j}.

We now choose

(5.65)

\displaystyle N(t):=-\eta\log_{2}t,\qquad\text{so that}\qquad t^{\eta/2}2^{N(t)/2}=1.

If
j\leq N(t)
, we use (
5.63
) and obtain

(5.66)

\displaystyle\sqrt{t}\,\|\nabla u_{j}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\lesssim t^{\eta/2}2^{N(t)/2}c_{j}=c_{j}.

If
j\geq N(t)
, we use (
5.64
) and obtain

(5.67)

\displaystyle\sqrt{t}\,\|\nabla u_{j}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\lesssim t^{-\eta/2}2^{-N(t)/2}c_{j}=c_{j}.

Therefore

(5.68)

\displaystyle\sqrt{t}\,\|\nabla u_{j}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\lesssim\,c_{j}.

Since
c_{j}\in\ell^{1}(\mathbb{Z})
and for each
j
we have (see (
4.72
))

(5.69)

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to 0}\sqrt{t}\,\|\nabla u_{j}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}=0

we conclude the continuity by dominated convergence.
∎

5.4.
Weak–strong uniqueness for Besov data

We start with the following lemma on
H^{-1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
-stability. It is inspired by
[
7
]
. We stress that here we consider two immediately strong solutions, not necessarily constructed as in Section 4, and therefore not enjoying the additional properties discussed there. We simply recall that, by
Definition

1.3
, there exist constants
C_{(\rho_{1},u_{1})}(u_{1})
and
C_{(\rho_{2},u_{2})}(u_{2})
such that

(5.70)

\displaystyle\sup_{i\in\{0,1,2,3\}}\sup_{t>0}A_{i}^{0}(t,u_{1})\leq C_{(\rho_{1},u_{1})}(u_{1}),\qquad\sup_{i\in\{0,1,2,3\}}\sup_{t>0}A_{i}^{0}(t,u_{2})\leq C_{(\rho_{2},u_{2})}(u_{2}).

Lemma 5.6
.

Let
(\rho_{1},u_{1})
and
(\rho_{2},u_{2})
be two immediately strong solutions of (
1.2
)
arising from the initial data
(\rho_{0},u_{1}(0))
and
(\rho_{0},u_{2}(0))
, both satisfying (

L
2

-patch
). Assume moreover that

(5.71)

L_{\infty}:=\exp\!\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}\left\lVert\nabla u_{2}(s)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\,\,\mathrm{d}s\right)<\infty.

Fix
T>0
. Then there exist constants
C,N>0
4
4

4

Here
N
depends on
\|u_{1}(0)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}
,
C_{(\rho_{1},u_{1})}(u_{1})
,
\|u_{2}(0)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}
,
C_{(\rho_{2},u_{2})}(u_{2})
,
T
, and
D
, while
C
depends on
N
and
L_{\infty}
.
such that

(5.72)

\supp\rho_{1}(t)\cup\supp\rho_{2}(t)\subset D_{N,t}:=D+B_{N\sqrt{t}}(0)\quad\text{for all }t\in(0,T),

and, for every
\phi\in H^{1}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
and every
t\in(0,T)
,

(5.73)

\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(\rho_{1}(t)-\rho_{2}(t))\,\phi\,\,\mathrm{d}x\right|\leq C\,t\sup_{s\in(0,t)}\left\lVert\rho_{1}(s)(u_{1}(s)-u_{2}(s))\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\,\left\lVert\nabla\phi\right\rVert_{L^{2}(D_{N,t})}.

Proof.

Applying
Proposition

3.9
to
(\rho_{1},u_{1})
we obtain that there exists

(5.74)

\displaystyle N_{1}=N_{1}\!\left(\|u_{1}(0)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})},C_{(\rho_{1},u_{1})}(u_{1}),T,D\right)

such that for every
t\in[0,T)

(5.75)

\displaystyle\supp\rho_{1}(t)\subset D_{N_{1},t}.

Applying
Proposition

3.9
also to
(\rho_{2},u_{2})
we obtain, for some
N_{2}>0
,

(5.76)

\displaystyle\supp\rho_{2}(t)\subset D_{N_{2},t}\qquad\text{for all }t\in[0,T).

Setting
N=\max\{N_{1},N_{2}\}
yields (
5.72
).

To prove (
5.73
) we use assumption (
5.71
).
If
X_{2}
denotes the flow map associated with
u_{2}
, then

(5.77)

\displaystyle\sup_{t>0}\left\lVert\nabla X_{2}(t)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq L_{\infty}.

Denoting
(\delta\rho,\delta u)=(\rho_{1}-\rho_{2},u_{1}-u_{2})
we observe that
\delta\rho
satisfies

(5.78)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\delta\rho+\dive(\delta\rho\,u_{2})=-\dive(\rho_{1}\delta u),\qquad\delta\rho(0)=0.

For every
\phi\in H^{1}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
we use the Lagrangian formulation of (
5.78
) (this computation can be justified by mollification and a commutator estimate; see
[
7
, Prop. 4.4]
):

(5.79)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\delta\rho(t,x)\phi(x)\,\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\int_{0}^{t}\dive(\rho_{1}\delta u)(s,X_{2}(s,x))\,\,\mathrm{d}s\;\phi(X_{2}(t,x))\,\,\mathrm{d}x.

Using integration by parts we obtain

(5.80)

\displaystyle\left\lvert\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\delta\rho(t,x)\phi(x)\,\,\mathrm{d}x\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{t}\left\lVert\rho_{1}\delta u(s)\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\left\lVert\nabla[\phi(X_{2}(t,\cdot))]\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\supp(\rho_{1}(s)))}\,\,\mathrm{d}s

(5.81)

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{s\in(0,t)}\left\lVert\rho_{1}\delta u(s)\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\int_{0}^{t}\left\lVert\nabla[\phi(X_{2}(t,\cdot))]\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\supp(\rho_{1}(s)))}\,\,\mathrm{d}s.

Let
X_{1}
denote the flow map associated with
u_{1}
.
Since
X_{1}(s,D)=\supp(\rho_{1}(s))
, we have

(5.82)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\nabla[\phi(X_{2}(t,\cdot))]\right\rVert_{L^{2}(X_{1}(s,D))}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert(\nabla\phi)(X_{2}(t,\cdot))\right\rVert_{L^{2}(X_{1}(s,D))}\left\lVert\nabla X_{2}(t)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

(5.83)

\displaystyle\leq L_{\infty}\left\lVert\nabla\phi\right\rVert_{L^{2}(X_{2}(t,X_{1}(s,D)))}.

Now let
x\in D
. Using (
5.72
) and
s\leq t
we estimate

(5.84)

\displaystyle|X_{2}(t,X_{1}(s,x))-x|

\displaystyle\leq|X_{2}(t,X_{1}(s,x))-X_{2}(t,x)|+|X_{2}(t,x)-x|

(5.85)

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\nabla X_{2}(t)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}|X_{1}(s,x)-x|+N_{2}\sqrt{t}

(5.86)

\displaystyle\leq L_{\infty}N_{1}\sqrt{t}+N_{2}\sqrt{t}.

Hence, up to redefining
N
, we obtain

(5.87)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\nabla\phi\right\rVert_{L^{2}(X_{2}(t,X_{1}(s,D)))}\leq\left\lVert\nabla\phi\right\rVert_{L^{2}(D_{N,t})}.

Inserting this estimate into the previous inequality yields (
5.73
).
∎

We now state the uniqueness result in the Besov setting. This is a weak–strong uniqueness result: the solution
(\rho_{2},u_{2})
has Besov regularity, while
(\rho_{1},u_{1})
is only immediately strong and satisfies the regularity estimates propagated from
L^{2}
data. In addition, the result yields a quantitative stability estimate between the two solutions.

Theorem 5.7

(Uniqueness)
.

Assume that
\rho_{0}=\mathbf{1}_{D}
, where
D
is a bounded Lipschitz domain, and let

u_{1}(0)\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
and
u_{2}(0)\in\dot{B}^{0}_{2,1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
.
Let
(\rho_{1},u_{1})
and
(\rho_{2},u_{2})
be two immediately strong solutions of (
1.2
)
arising from the initial data
(\rho_{0},u_{1}(0))
and
(\rho_{0},u_{2}(0))
, respectively.

Then there exists a function
\gamma\in L^{1}(0,\infty)
such that, for almost every
t>0
, there exists a constant

(5.88)

C_{t}=C\!\left(t,\|u_{1}(0)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})},C_{(\rho_{1},u_{1})}(u_{1}),\|u_{2}(0)\|_{\dot{B}^{0}_{2,1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})},D\right),

5
5

5

One can verify that
C_{t}
is non-decreasing with respect to
\|u_{1}(0)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}
,
C_{(\rho_{1},u_{1})}(u_{1})
, and
\|u_{2}(0)\|_{\dot{B}^{0}_{2,1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}
.

for which

(5.89)

\sup_{s\in(0,t)}\|\rho_{1}(s)[u_{1}(s)-u_{2}(s)]\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\|\nabla\delta u(s)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}\,\,\mathrm{d}s\leq\|\rho_{0}[u_{1}(0)-u_{2}(0)]\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}\exp\!\Bigl(C_{t}\int_{0}^{t}\gamma(s)\,\,\mathrm{d}s\Bigr).

In particular, if
u_{1}(0)=u_{2}(0)
, then
(\rho_{1},u_{1})=(\rho_{2},u_{2})
almost everywhere in
(0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
.

Proof.

Throughout the proof, any constant with the dependence specified in (
5.88
) will be denoted by
C_{t}
adn frequently omiteed by
\lesssim
.

Without loss of generality, we assume that
(\rho_{2},u_{2})
is constructed as in
Proposition

4.1
and satisfies all the properties stated in
Proposition

5.5
. In particular, thanks to
Remark

4.3
, there exists a constant
C
depending only on
\|u_{2}(0)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}
such that

(5.90)

\displaystyle C_{(\rho_{2},u_{2})}(u_{2})=C\bigl(\|u_{2}(0)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\bigr)\leq C\bigl(\|u_{2}(0)\|_{\dot{B}^{0}_{2,1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\bigr)\lesssim C_{t}.

Set

(5.91)

\displaystyle\delta\rho\coloneqq\rho_{1}-\rho_{2},\qquad\delta u\coloneqq u_{1}-u_{2}.

For convenience, introduce

(5.92)

\displaystyle f(s)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sup_{\tau\in(0,s)}\|\rho_{1}(\tau)\,\delta u(\tau)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})},\qquad g(s)\coloneqq\|\nabla\delta u(s)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}.

By
Proposition

5.5
, the solution
(\rho_{2},u_{2})
has sufficient regularity to apply

Theorem

A.5
with
v_{1}=u_{1}
and
v_{2}=u_{2}
. Therefore, for almost every
t>0
, we obtain

(5.93)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}f^{2}(t)+\int_{0}^{t}g(s)^{2}\,\,\mathrm{d}s\leq\frac{1}{2}\|\rho_{0}\,\delta u(0)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}I(s)\,\,\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}J(s)\,\,\mathrm{d}s,

where

(5.94)

\displaystyle I(s)\coloneqq\Bigl|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho_{1}\,\delta u\otimes\delta u:\nabla u_{2}\Bigr|,\qquad J(s)\coloneqq\Bigl|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\delta\rho\,\delta u\cdot\dot{u}_{2}\Bigr|.

Estimates on
\gamma
.

Before studying (
5.93
) and attempting to recover a Gronwall-type inequality, we show that

(5.95)

\displaystyle\gamma(s)\coloneqq\|\nabla u_{2}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+s^{\frac{5}{2}}\,\|\nabla\dot{u}_{2}(s)\|_{L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}+s\,\|\rho_{2}(s)\,\dot{u}_{2}(s)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}+s^{\frac{3}{4}}\,\|\nabla\dot{u}_{2}(s)\|_{L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

belongs to
L^{1}(0,\infty)
.

The first and the last terms belong to
L^{1}(0,\infty)
thanks to
Proposition

5.5
.
The third term belongs to
L^{1}(0,\infty)
thanks to the bound
A_{1}(\rho_{2},u_{2})
.
It remains to control the second term.

Using the Gagliardo–Nirenberg inequality

(5.96)

\displaystyle\|\nabla\dot{u}_{2}\|_{L^{4}}^{2}\lesssim\|\nabla\dot{u}_{2}\|_{L^{2}}\,\|\nabla^{2}\dot{u}_{2}\|_{L^{2}},

we obtain

(5.97)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}s^{\frac{5}{2}}\,\|\nabla\dot{u}_{2}(s)\|_{L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}\,\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}s^{\frac{5}{2}}\,\|\nabla\dot{u}_{2}(s)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\,\|\nabla^{2}\dot{u}_{2}(s)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\,\,\mathrm{d}s

(5.98)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{t}s^{2}\,\|\nabla\dot{u}_{2}(s)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}\,\,\mathrm{d}s+\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{t}s^{3}\,\|\nabla^{2}\dot{u}_{2}(s)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}\,\,\mathrm{d}s.

Both terms are finite thanks to
A_{2}(\rho_{2},u_{2})
and
A_{3}(\rho_{2},u_{2})
.
Therefore,
\gamma\in L^{1}(0,\infty)
.

Estimate of
I(s)
.

By Hölder’s inequality we obtain

(5.99)

\displaystyle I(s)\leq\|\rho_{1}(s)\,\delta u(s)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}\|\nabla u_{2}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq f(s)^{2}\,\gamma(s).

Estimate of
J(s)
.
Since
\nabla u_{2}\in L^{1}((0,\infty);L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2}))
, we may apply
Lemma

5.6
with

(5.100)

\displaystyle\phi=\delta u(s)\cdot\dot{u}_{2}(s)

to obtain the existence of
N>0
such that, for almost every
s\in(0,t)
,

(5.101)

\displaystyle J(s)\leq C_{t}\,s\,\sup_{\tau\in(0,s)}\|\rho_{1}(\tau)\,\delta u(\tau)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\,\|\nabla(\delta u(s)\cdot\dot{u}_{2}(s))\|_{L^{2}(D_{N,s})},

and

(5.102)

\displaystyle\supp\rho_{1}(\tau)\cup\supp\rho_{2}(\tau)\subset D_{N,s}\qquad\text{for all }\tau\in(0,s).

Using the product rule

(5.103)

\displaystyle\nabla(\delta u\cdot\dot{u}_{2})=(\nabla\delta u)\,\dot{u}_{2}+\delta u\cdot\nabla\dot{u}_{2},

and (
5.92
), we deduce from (
5.101
) that

(5.104)

\displaystyle J(s)

\displaystyle\lesssim s\,f(s)\,g(s)\,\|\dot{u}_{2}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}(D_{N,s})}+s\,f(s)\,\|\delta u(s)\|_{L^{4}(D_{N,s})}\,\|\nabla\dot{u}_{2}(s)\|_{L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}.

By
Corollary

3.6
applied to
(\rho_{1},u_{1})
with
v=\delta u(s)
and
R=N
, we obtain for a.e.
s\in(0,t)

(5.105)

\displaystyle s^{\frac{1}{4}}\,\|\delta u(s)\|_{L^{4}(D_{N,s})}\;\lesssim\;s^{\frac{1}{2}}\,\|\nabla\delta u(s)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\|\rho_{1}(s)\,\delta u(s)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}=s^{\frac{1}{2}}g(s)+f(s).

With the same argument applied to
(\rho_{2},u_{2})
with
v=\dot{u}_{2}(s)
(and the same
R=N
), we obtain

(5.106)

\displaystyle s^{\frac{1}{2}}\,\|\dot{u}_{2}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}(D_{N,s})}\;\lesssim\;s^{\frac{3}{4}}\,\|\nabla\dot{u}_{2}(s)\|_{L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\|\rho_{2}(s)\,\dot{u}_{2}(s)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}.

Inserting (
5.105
)–(
5.106
) into (
5.104
) yields

(5.107)

\displaystyle J(s)

\displaystyle\leq C_{t}\,f(s)\,s\,g(s)\Bigl[s^{\frac{1}{4}}\|\nabla\dot{u}_{2}(s)\|_{L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+s^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|\rho_{2}(s)\,\dot{u}_{2}(s)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\Bigr]

(5.108)

\displaystyle\quad+C_{t}\,f(s)\,s^{\frac{3}{4}}\Bigl[s^{\frac{1}{2}}g(s)+f(s)\Bigr]\,\|\nabla\dot{u}_{2}(s)\|_{L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}.

Finally, by Young’s inequality, we obtain (up to redefining
C_{t}
)

(5.109)

\displaystyle J(s)\leq\frac{1}{2}g(s)^{2}+C_{t}\,f(s)^{2}\,\gamma(s).

Conclusion (Gronwall).

Combining (
5.93
), (
5.99
), and (
5.109
), and absorbing the factor
g(s)^{2}
, we obtain for a.e.
t>0
,

(5.110)

\displaystyle f(t)^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}g(s)^{2}\,\,\mathrm{d}s\leq 2\|\rho_{0}\,\delta u(0)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}+C_{t}\int_{0}^{t}\gamma(s)\,f(s)^{2}\,\,\mathrm{d}s.

Now we define the relative energy as

(5.111)

\displaystyle E_{\operatorname{rel}}(t)=\sup_{s\in(0,t)}\|\rho_{1}(s)\,\delta u(s)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\|\nabla\delta u(s)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}\,\,\mathrm{d}s.

Since
f(t)^{2}\leq 2E_{\mathrm{rel}}(t)
, we obtain from (
5.110
)

(5.112)

\displaystyle E_{\operatorname{rel}}(t)\leq 2\|\rho_{0}\,\delta u(0)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}+C_{t}\int_{0}^{t}\gamma(s)\,E_{\operatorname{rel}}(s)\,\,\mathrm{d}s.

By Gronwall’s lemma we conclude

(5.113)

\displaystyle E_{\operatorname{rel}}(t)\leq\|\rho_{0}\,\delta u(0)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}\exp\!\Bigl(C_{t}\int_{0}^{t}\gamma(s)\,\,\mathrm{d}s\Bigr).

If the two initial data coincide, we obtain that

(5.114)

\displaystyle\|\nabla\delta u(s)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}=0\quad\text{for a.e. }s\in(0,t),

which implies that

(5.115)

\displaystyle u_{1}(s,x)-u_{2}(s,x)=c(s)

for some function
c:(0,t)\to\mathbb{R}^{2}
. Moreover,

(5.116)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\sup_{s\in(0,t)}\|\rho_{1}(s)\,\delta u(s)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}=\sup_{s\in(0,t)}|c(s)|\,\|\rho_{1}(s)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}=|D|^{1/2}\sup_{s\in(0,t)}|c(s)|.

This implies that
c(s)=0
for almost every
s\in(0,t)
, and therefore

(5.117)

\displaystyle u_{1}=u_{2}\quad\text{a.e. in }(0,t)\times\mathbb{R}^{2}.

By the freedom in the choice of
t>0
we conclude

(5.118)

\displaystyle u_{1}=u_{2}\quad\text{a.e. in }(0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{2}.

∎

6.
Additional properties of Besov solutions

In this section, we study properties of the unique solution
(\rho,u)
associated with the initial data

\rho_{0}=\mathbf{1}_{D}
and
u_{0}\in\dot{B}^{0}_{2,1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
, constructed in
Proposition

4.6
.

By
Proposition

5.5
, this solution is Lipschitz. Denoting by
X
the measure-preserving flow associated with
u
, we have

(6.1)

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in(0,\infty)}\|\nabla X(t)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq\exp\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}\|\nabla u(t)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\,\,\mathrm{d}t\right)=:L_{\infty}.

This estimate allows us to apply Gagliardo–Nirenberg-type inequalities on the evolving domains
D_{t}=X(t,D)
. We only state the specific cases needed in the sequel.

Lemma 6.1
.

Let
(\rho,u)
be the solution constructed in
Proposition

4.1
, and assume in addition that
u_{0}\in\dot{B}^{0}_{2,1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
. Denote by
X(t)
its flow, and let
p\in[2,\infty)
.
Then there exists a constant

(6.2)

\displaystyle C=C\bigl(p,\|u_{0}\|_{\dot{B}^{0}_{2,1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})},D\bigr)

such that for almost every
t>0
and every
v(t)\in L^{2}(D_{t})\cap\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
, we have

(6.3)

\displaystyle\|v(t)-M_{t}\|_{L^{p}(D_{t})}\leq C\|v(t)-M_{t}\|_{L^{2}(D_{t})}^{2/p}\|\nabla v(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{1-2/p},\qquad M_{t}\coloneqq\fint_{D_{t}}v(t,x)\,\,\mathrm{d}x.

Similarly, there exists a constant
C=C(\|u_{0}\|_{\dot{B}^{0}_{2,1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})},D)
such that

(6.4)

\displaystyle\|v(t)-M_{t}\|_{L^{\infty}(D_{t})}\leq C\|v(t)-M_{t}\|_{L^{2}(D_{t})}^{1/2}\|\nabla v(t)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{1/2}.

Proof.

Fix
t>0
. Since
X(t,\cdot)
is bi-Lipschitz with distortion uniformly bounded by
L_{\infty}
, the domain
D_{t}=X(t,D)
is Lipschitz with uniformly controlled character. Hence, there exists an extension operator

(6.5)

\displaystyle E_{t}:H^{1}(D_{t})\to H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2}),\qquad E_{t}:W^{1,\infty}(D_{t})\to W^{1,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2}),

whose operator norms are bounded independently of
t
. Set

(6.6)

\displaystyle f_{t}(x)\coloneqq v(t,x)-M_{t},\qquad x\in D_{t}.

Then
\int_{D_{t}}f_{t}\,\,\mathrm{d}x=0
. By the boundedness of
E_{t}
, the Gagliardo–Nirenberg inequality on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
, and the Poincaré inequality on
D_{t}
, we obtain

(6.7)

\displaystyle\|f_{t}\|_{L^{p}(D_{t})}

\displaystyle\leq\|E_{t}f_{t}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\lesssim\|E_{t}f_{t}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2/p}\|\nabla E_{t}f_{t}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{1-2/p}\lesssim\|f_{t}\|_{L^{2}(D_{t})}^{2/p}\|\nabla f_{t}\|_{L^{2}(D_{t})}^{1-2/p}.

Since
\nabla f_{t}=\nabla v(t)
on
D_{t}
, this yields the first estimate.

The
L^{\infty}
case follows in the same way, using the corresponding Gagliardo–Nirenberg inequality on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
:

(6.8)

\displaystyle\|f_{t}\|_{L^{\infty}(D_{t})}\lesssim\|f_{t}\|_{L^{2}(D_{t})}^{1/2}\|\nabla f_{t}\|_{L^{\infty}(D_{t})}^{1/2},

which gives (
6.4
).
∎

6.1.
C^{1,\gamma}
patches

As in
[
15
]
, we can show that the flow is uniformly
C^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
which implies that
C^{1}
patches are preserved under flow. More precisely, we have the following.

Proposition 6.2
.

Let
(\rho,u)
be the solution constructed in
Proposition

4.1
, and assume in addition that
u_{0}\in\dot{B}^{0}_{2,1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
. Then there exists a constant
C>0
such that

(6.9)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\left\lVert\nabla u(t)\right\rVert_{\dot{B}^{1/2}_{4,1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\,\mathrm{d}t\leq C.

In particular, for every
t\geq 0,
we have
X(t)\in C^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
and, if
D
is a
C^{1}
-domain, then
D_{t}
is a
C^{1}
-domain as well.

Proof.

Every constant that may depend on
\left\lVert u_{0}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}
,
\left\lVert u_{0}\right\rVert_{\dot{B}^{0}_{2,1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}
and
D
is omitted.

Since
\dot{B}^{1/2}_{4,1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\hookrightarrow C_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
by
[
4
, Proposition 2.39]
, it suffices to show (
6.9
) to prove
Proposition

6.2
. In order to prove (
6.9
), recall that

(6.10)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \left\lVert\nabla u(t)\right\rVert_{\dot{B}^{1/2}_{4,1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\lesssim&\|\nabla u(t)\|_{L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{1/2}\|\nabla^{2}u(t)\|_{L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{1/2}\\
\lesssim&t^{-1/8}\|\nabla u(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{1/4}\|\nabla^{2}u(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{1/4}\|\sqrt{\rho}\dot{u}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{1/4}t^{1/8}\|\nabla\dot{u}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{1/4}\end{aligned}

In the last step, we used the Stokes estimates, that
\sqrt{\rho}\dot{u}
is mean free thanks to
Lemma

3.11
and
Lemma

6.1
, i.e.,

(6.11)

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{2}u(t)\|_{L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\lesssim\|\sqrt{\rho}\dot{u}(t)\|_{L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\lesssim\|\sqrt{\rho}\dot{u}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{1/2}\|\nabla\dot{u}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{1/2}

We see that each factor appearing on the right-hand side of (
6.10
) is in
L^{4}((0,\infty))
thanks to
Proposition

5.5
and we deduce (
6.9
) which completes the proof.
∎

Higher regularity of the patch is propagated under suitable assumptions on the initial velocity.

Proposition 6.3
.

Let
(\rho,u)
be the solution constructed in
Proposition

4.1
, and assume in addition that
u_{0}\in\dot{B}^{0}_{2,1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
. If additionally
u_{0}\in\dot{B}^{\gamma}_{2,1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
, where
\gamma\in(0,1),
then there exists a constant
C>0
such that

(6.12)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\left\lVert\nabla u(t)\right\rVert_{C^{\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\,\mathrm{d}t\leq C\left(\left\lVert u_{0}\right\rVert_{\dot{B}^{0}_{2,1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\left\lVert u_{0}\right\rVert_{\dot{B}^{\gamma}_{2,1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\right).

In particular, for every
t\geq 0,
we have that
X(t)\in C^{1,\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
and, if
D
is a
C^{1,\gamma}
-domain, then
D_{t}
is a
C^{1,\gamma}
-domain as well.

We recall the definition of the
C^{\gamma}
norm for
\gamma\in(0,1)
:

(6.13)

\displaystyle\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{C^{\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\coloneqq\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\left\lvert f\right\rvert_{C^{\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{2})},\qquad\text{where }\quad\left\lvert f\right\rvert_{C^{\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\coloneqq\sup_{x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{2}}\frac{\left\lvert f(x)-f(y)\right\rvert}{\left\lvert x-y\right\rvert^{\gamma}}.

Proof.

Every constant that may depend on
\left\lVert u_{0}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}
,
\left\lVert u_{0}\right\rVert_{\dot{B}^{0}_{2,1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}
and
D
is omitted.

Again it suffices to show (
6.12
) in order to prove
Proposition

6.3
. Since

(6.14)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\|\nabla u(t)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\,\mathrm{d}t\leq C

by
Proposition

5.5
it only remains to show that

(6.15)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}|\nabla u(t)|_{C^{\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\,\mathrm{d}t\lesssim\left\lVert u_{0}\right\rVert_{\dot{B}^{\gamma}_{2,1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

To this end, recall that
\dot{B}^{\gamma}_{2,1}=[L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2}),\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})]_{\gamma,1}
which implies that there is a sequence
(u_{0,j})_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\subset L_{\sigma}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\cap\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
such that

(6.16)

\displaystyle u_{0}=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}u_{0,j},\quad\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{\gamma j}\|u_{0,j}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+2^{-(1-\gamma)j}\|u_{0,j}\|_{\dot{H^{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq 2\left\lVert u_{0}\right\rVert_{\dot{B}^{\gamma}_{2,1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}.

We fix
j\in\mathbb{Z}
and let
u_{j}
be a solution of the linearized system (
4.60
) with initial value
u_{0,j}.

First, we show that, for
p=2/(1-\gamma)
,

(6.17)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\|\sqrt{\rho}\dot{u}_{j}(t)\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\,\mathrm{d}t\lesssim 2^{\gamma j}\|u_{0,j}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+2^{-(1-\gamma)j}\|u_{0,j}\|_{\dot{H^{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}.

Then, by linear Stokes estimates and Morrey’s inequality,

(6.18)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}|\nabla u_{j}(t)|_{C^{\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\,\mathrm{d}t\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\|\nabla^{2}u_{j}(t)\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\,\mathrm{d}t\lesssim\int_{0}^{\infty}\|\sqrt{\rho}\dot{u}_{j}(t)\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\,\mathrm{d}t

(6.19)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle 2^{\gamma j}\|u_{0,j}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+2^{-(1-\gamma)j}\|u_{0,j}\|_{\dot{H^{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}.

Summing over
j\in\mathbb{Z}
implies the thesis. In order to show (
6.17
), observe that, due to
Lemma

6.1
, for every
t>0,

(6.20)

\displaystyle\|\sqrt{\rho}\dot{u}_{j}(t)\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\lesssim\|\sqrt{\rho}\dot{u}_{j}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{1-\gamma}\|\nabla\dot{u}_{j}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{\gamma}.

Thanks to
Theorem

2.2
, for almost every
t>0,

(6.21)

\displaystyle\|\sqrt{\rho}\dot{u}_{j}(t)\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\sqrt{\rho}\dot{u}_{j}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{1-\gamma}\|\nabla\dot{u}_{j}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{\gamma}

(6.22)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left(\|\sqrt{\rho_{0}}u_{0,j}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}t^{-1}\right)^{1-\gamma}\left(\|\sqrt{\rho_{0}}u_{0,j}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}t^{-3/2}\right)^{\gamma}\lesssim\|u_{0}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}t^{-1-\gamma/2},

and that, for every
j\in\mathbb{Z}
and almost every
t>0,

(6.23)

\displaystyle\|\sqrt{\rho}\dot{u}_{j}(t)\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\sqrt{\rho}\dot{u}_{j}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{1-\gamma}\|\nabla\dot{u}_{j}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{\gamma}

(6.24)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left(\|\nabla u_{0,j}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}t^{-1/2}\right)^{1-\gamma}\left(\|\nabla u_{0,j}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}t^{-1}\right)^{\gamma}\lesssim\|\nabla u_{0}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}t^{-1/2-\gamma/2}.

Consequently, for every
j\in\mathbb{Z}
and every
t>0,

(6.25)

\displaystyle\|\sqrt{\rho}\dot{u}_{j}(t)\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\lesssim\min\left\{\|u_{0}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}t^{-1-\gamma/2},\|\nabla u_{0}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}t^{-1+\gamma/2}\right\}.

We infer that, for every
j\in\mathbb{Z}
and every
A>0,

(6.26)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\|\sqrt{\rho}\dot{u}_{j}(t)\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\,\mathrm{d}s\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\nabla u_{0}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\int_{0}^{A}t^{-1/2-\gamma/2}\,\mathrm{d}s+\|u_{0}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\int_{A}^{\infty}t^{-1-\gamma/2}\,\mathrm{d}s

(6.27)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\nabla u_{0}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}A^{1/2-\gamma/2}+\|u_{0}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}A^{-\gamma/2}.

We choose
A=2^{-2j}
and obtain (
6.17
) which completes the proof.
∎

6.2.
Asymptotics.

By
Lemma

3.11
, the average momentum of
u
on the patch is conserved for all times:

(6.28)

\displaystyle M\coloneqq\frac{1}{|D|}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho_{0}(x)u_{0}(x)\,\,\mathrm{d}x=\frac{1}{|D_{t}|}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho(t,x)u(t,x)\,\,\mathrm{d}x=\fint_{D_{t}}u(t,x)\,\,\mathrm{d}x.

The combination of finite distorsion (
6.1
), (
6.28
), and the energy equality allows us to prove exponential decay of
u(t)-M
on the patch
D_{t}
in the
L^{2}
norm. To track this decay, we do not assume
\nu=1
, unlike in the rest of the paper, and instead keep the viscosity explicit.

Lemma 6.4
.

In the previous setup, we have

(6.29)

\displaystyle\|u(t)-M\|_{L^{2}(D_{t})}\leq\|u_{0}-M\|_{L^{2}(D)}\,e^{-\lambda t},\qquad\lambda\coloneqq\frac{\nu}{C_{D}^{2}L_{\infty}^{2}},

where
C_{D}
is the Poincaré constant of
D
.

Proof.

We introduce the Galilean transform

(6.30)

\displaystyle\rho_{M}(t,x)\coloneqq\rho(t,x+Mt),\qquad u_{M}(t,x)\coloneqq u(t,x+Mt)-M.

Since the system is Galilean invariant,
(\rho_{M},u_{M})
satisfies the same equations as
(\rho,u)
, and therefore for every
t>s\geq 0
we have the energy equality

(6.31)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\|\sqrt{\rho_{M}}u_{M}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}+\nu\int_{s}^{t}\|\nabla u_{M}(\tau)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}\,\,\mathrm{d}\tau=\frac{1}{2}\|\sqrt{\rho_{M}}u_{M}(s)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}

Using the change of variables
y=x+Mt
and the invariance of spatial derivatives under translations, this identity becomes

(6.32)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\|u(t)-M\|_{L^{2}(D_{t})}^{2}+\nu\int_{s}^{t}\|\nabla u(\tau)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}\,\,\mathrm{d}\tau=\frac{1}{2}\|u(s)-M\|_{L^{2}(D_{s})}^{2}.

By a change of variables using
D_{\tau}=X(\tau,D)

(6.33)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \|u(\tau)-M\|_{L^{2}(D_{\tau})}&=\Big\|u(\tau,\cdot)-\fint_{D_{\tau}}u(\tau,x)\,\,\mathrm{d}x\Big\|_{L^{2}(D_{\tau})}\\
&=\Big\|u(\tau,X(\tau,\cdot))-\fint_{D}u(\tau,X(\tau,y))\,\,\mathrm{d}y\Big\|_{L^{2}(D)}\end{aligned}

By Poincaré inequality on
D
and recalling that
\|\nabla X(t)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq L_{\infty}
, we infer that

(6.34)

\displaystyle\Big\|u(\tau,X(\tau,\cdot))-\fint_{D}u(\tau,X(\tau,y))\,\,\mathrm{d}y\Big\|_{L^{2}(D)}

\displaystyle\leq C_{D}\|\nabla(u(\tau)\circ X(\tau,\cdot))\|_{L^{2}(D)}

(6.35)

\displaystyle\leq C_{D}\|\nabla u(\tau)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\|\nabla X(\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

(6.36)

\displaystyle\leq C_{D}L_{\infty}\|\nabla u(\tau)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}.

We have proved

(6.37)

\displaystyle\|u(\tau)-M\|_{L^{2}(D_{\tau})}\leq C_{D}L_{\infty}\|\nabla u(\tau)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}.

Therefore,

(6.38)

\displaystyle\|\nabla u(\tau)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}\geq\frac{1}{C_{D}^{2}L_{\infty}^{2}}\|u(\tau)-M\|_{L^{2}(D_{\tau})}^{2}.

Setting

(6.39)

\displaystyle F(t)\coloneqq\frac{1}{2}\|u(t)-M\|_{L^{2}(D_{t})}^{2},

and inserting (
6.38
) into (
6.32
) gives for every
t>s\geq 0

(6.40)

\displaystyle F(t)+2\lambda\int_{s}^{t}F(\tau)\,\,\mathrm{d}\tau\leq F(s).

By Gronwall’s lemma, it follows that

(6.41)

\displaystyle F(t)\leq F(0)e^{-2\lambda t}.

Taking square roots, we obtain the thesis.
∎

Lemma 6.5
.

In the previous setting, there exists a measure-preserving map
X_{\infty}
such that

(6.42)

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to\infty}\bigl\|(X(t,\cdot)-Mt)-X_{\infty}(\cdot)\bigr\|_{L^{\infty}(D)}=0.

Moreover, for every
x\in D
, we have

(6.43)

\displaystyle X_{\infty}(x)=x+\int_{0}^{\infty}\bigl(u(t,X(t,x))-M\bigr)\,\,\mathrm{d}t,

where

(6.44)

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in D}|u(t,X(t,x))-M|\in L^{1}(0,\infty).

Proof.

The goal is to show that

(6.45)

\displaystyle\bigl(X(t,\cdot)-Mt\bigr)_{t>0}\qquad\text{is Cauchy in }L^{\infty}(D),

which immediately implies (
6.42
). For every
x\in D
and
0\leq s<t
, we have

(6.46)

\displaystyle|X(t,x)-Mt-(X(s,x)-Ms)|

\displaystyle=\left|\int_{s}^{t}\bigl(u(\tau,X(\tau,x))-M\bigr)\,\,\mathrm{d}\tau\right|\leq\int_{s}^{t}\|u(\tau)-M\|_{L^{\infty}(D_{\tau})}\,\,\mathrm{d}\tau.

By (
6.4
) with
v=u
ther exist a constant
C=C(\left\lVert u_{0}\right\rVert_{\dot{B}^{0}_{2,1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})},D)
such that

(6.47)

\displaystyle\|u(\tau)-M\|_{L^{\infty}(D_{\tau})}\leq C\|u(\tau)-M\|_{L^{2}(D_{\tau})}^{\frac{1}{2}}\,\|\nabla u(\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Putting together (
6.46
) and (
6.47
), and using
Lemma

6.4
, we obtain

(6.48)

\displaystyle\left\lVert X(t,x)-Mt-(X(s,x)-Ms)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(D)}

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{s}^{t}\|u(\tau)-M\|_{L^{2}(D_{\tau})}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla u(\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{\frac{1}{2}}\,\,\mathrm{d}\tau

(6.49)

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{s}^{t}e^{-\frac{\lambda\tau}{2}}\|\nabla u(\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{\frac{1}{2}}\,\,\mathrm{d}\tau.

Since
\nabla u\in L^{1}((0,\infty);L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2}))
, Hölder’s inequality yields

(6.50)

\displaystyle H(\tau)\coloneqq e^{-\frac{\lambda\tau}{2}}\|\nabla u(\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{\frac{1}{2}}\in L^{1}(0,\infty).

Therefore,

(6.51)

\displaystyle\left\lVert X(t,x)-Mt-(X(s,x)-Ms)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(D)}\leq\int_{s}^{t}H(\tau)\,\,\mathrm{d}\tau,

which proves (
6.45
) and (
6.44
). The solution formula (
6.43
) is obatine dy passing to the limit for
t\to\infty
the equality

(6.52)

\displaystyle X(t,x)-Mt

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\bigl(u(\tau,X(\tau,x))-M\bigr)\,\,\mathrm{d}\tau.

∎

Remark 6.6
.

The last estimate of the previous lemma implies that there exists a
bi-Lipschitz and measure-preserving map
X_{\infty}
such that

(6.53)

\displaystyle\|X(t,\cdot)-Mt-X_{\infty}(\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}(D)}\to 0\qquad\text{as }t\to\infty.

In particular,

(6.54)

\displaystyle D_{t}-Mt=X(t,D)-Mt\to X_{\infty}(D)\eqqcolon D_{\infty}.

Comments.

(1)

We have shown that, for large times, the patch converges to a Lipschitz domain
D_{\infty}
which moves with constant velocity
M
(the average initial momentum). In other words, viscosity gradually removes the deformations of the patch, and the only motion that remains is a rigid translation. This behavior is expected: viscosity smooths the velocity field, while the conservation of momentum prevents the flow from stopping.

(2)

Notice that
L_{\infty}
is dimensionless and therefore

(6.55)

\displaystyle\lambda\approx\frac{\nu}{C_{D}^{2}}\approx\frac{\nu}{\operatorname{diam}(D)^{2}}\approx\left[\frac{1}{T}\right].

Hence the characteristic time scale of the system is

(6.56)

\displaystyle T_{*}\sim\frac{1}{\lambda}\sim\frac{\operatorname{diam}(D)^{2}}{\nu}.

This is the time needed for viscosity to smooth the velocity inside the patch. For
t\ll T_{*}
the velocity may still vary significantly in the patch, while for
t\gg T_{*}
these variations become small and the motion approaches the asymptotic regime described above. This time scale is the same as the classical diffusive time scale of the heat equation.

Appendix A
The relative energy inequality

A.1.
The linear case

We first study the following scenario.

Scenario 1
.

(i)

(\rho,u)
is an immediately strong solution of (
1.2
)
with initial data
(\rho_{0},u_{0})
satisfying (

L
2

-patch
);

(ii)

v
is an immediately strong solution of the linearized system
(
2.1
) advected by
(\rho,u)
, such that

\rho v\to\rho_{0}v_{0}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
.

Theorem A.1
.

Under the assumptions of Scenario 1, for almost every
t>0
we have

(A.1)

\displaystyle\|\rho(t)\,(u(t)-v(t))\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}+2\int_{0}^{t}\|\nabla(u(s)-v(s))\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}\,ds\leq\|\rho_{0}\,(u_{0}-v_{0})\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}.

Proof.

Set
w:=u-v
.
Using the energy inequalities for
u
and
v
, yields

\displaystyle\|\rho(t)w(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}

\displaystyle=\|\rho(t)u(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}-2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho(t)\,u(t)\cdot v(t)\,dx+\|\rho(t)v(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|\rho_{0}u_{0}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}+\|\rho_{0}v_{0}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}-2\int_{0}^{t}\Bigl(\|\nabla u(s)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}+\|\nabla v(s)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}\Bigr)\,ds

(A.2)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt-2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho(t)\,u(t)\cdot v(t)\,dx.

Using
Proposition

A.4
, we infer that for almost every
t>0
,

(A.3)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho(t)\,u(t)\cdot v(t)\,dx-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho_{0}\,u_{0}\cdot v_{0}\,dx=-2\int_{0}^{t}\!\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\nabla u(s,x):\nabla v(s,x)\,dx\,ds.

Inserting (
A.3
) into the previous inequality and rearranging the terms we conclude the proof.
∎

To show
Proposition

A.4
we need some preliminary result. We first show an admissibility result for the transport equation.

Proposition A.2
.

Assume that
(\rho,u)
is an immediately strong solution of (
1.2
) with initial data satisfying (

L
2

-patch
). Let
\varphi\in W^{1,1}_{\mathrm{loc}}((0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{2})
be such that

(A.4)

\displaystyle\rho\varphi,\ \rho\nabla\varphi\in L_{\mathrm{loc}}^{1}([0,\infty);L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})),\quad\ \rho\,\partial_{t}\varphi\in L_{\mathrm{loc}}^{1}([0,\infty);L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})),\quad\rho\varphi\in C([0,\infty);L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})).

Then, for almost every
t>0
, we have

(A.5)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho(t)\,\varphi(t)-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho_{0}\,\varphi(0)=\int_{0}^{t}\!\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho\,\partial_{t}\varphi+\rho\,u\cdot\nabla\varphi.

Proof.

Fix
{\varepsilon}>0
and let
\eta\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
be such that
0\leq\eta\leq 1
,

\eta\equiv 1
on
B_{1}(0)
and
\supp\eta\subset B_{2}(0)
.
For
n\geq 1
set

(A.6)

\displaystyle\chi_{n}(x):=\eta\Bigl(\frac{x}{n}\Bigr),\qquad\varphi^{n}:=\varphi\,\chi_{n}.

By assumption we have
\varphi^{n}\in W^{1,1}((\varepsilon,\infty);W^{1,1}(\mathbb{R}^{2}))
and, as in
[
7
, Lemma 3.4]
, one can show that it is an admissible test function for the weak formulation of the
transport equation of
(\rho,u)
.
Therefore, for a.e.
s,t\in({\varepsilon},\infty)
,

(A.7)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho(t)\,\varphi^{n}(t)-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho(s)\,\varphi^{n}(s)=\int_{s}^{t}\!\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho\,\partial_{t}\varphi^{n}+\rho\,u\cdot\nabla\varphi^{n}.

By the continuity assumption on
\rho u
the left hand side of (
A.7
) converges to the left hand side of (
A.5
) for
n\to\infty
and
s\to 0
. Since
\rho\partial_{t}\varphi\in L^{1}_{t}L^{1}_{x}
also the first term of the right hand side converges. For the last term we have

(A.8)

\displaystyle\int_{s}^{t}\!\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho\,u\cdot(\nabla\varphi^{n}-\nabla\varphi)

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\rho u\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}((0,t);L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\left\lVert\rho\nabla(\varphi^{n}-\varphi)\right\rVert_{L^{1}((0,t);L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

The last factor is finite since
\rho\nabla\varphi\in L^{1}((0,t);L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2}))
.
Moreover, since
\rho(t)=\mathbf{1}_{D_{t}}
with
D_{t}
bounded, for
n
large enough we have
\chi_{n}\equiv 1
on
\supp\rho(s)
for all
s\in[0,t]
, hence

(A.9)

\displaystyle\rho\nabla(\varphi^{n}-\varphi)=\rho\,\varphi\nabla\chi_{n}.

Therefore,

(A.10)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\rho\nabla(\varphi^{n}-\varphi)\right\rVert_{L^{1}((0,t);L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2}))}\leq\frac{C}{n}\left\lVert\rho\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{1}((0,t);L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2}))}\to 0,

which concludes the proof.
∎

Proposition

A.2
allows us to show that in the first Scenario the admissibility of
u\cdot v
for the transport equation of
(\rho,u)
in the time interval
({\varepsilon},\infty)
.

Corollary A.3
.

Under the hypothesis of Scenario 1 for any
{\varepsilon}>0
and almost every
t\in({\varepsilon},\infty)
, we have

(A.11)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho(t)u(t)\cdot v(t)-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho({\varepsilon})u({\varepsilon})\cdot v({\varepsilon})=\int_{\varepsilon}^{t}\!\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho v\cdot\dot{u}+\rho u\cdot D_{u}(v).

Moreover we have the following convergence

(A.12)

\displaystyle\lim_{{\varepsilon}\to 0}\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho({\varepsilon})u({\varepsilon})\cdot v({\varepsilon})=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho_{0}u_{0}\cdot v_{0}.

Proof.

We simply check the assumptions of
Proposition

A.2
to show that
u\cdot v
is an admissible test function.
By
Lemma

3.10

u\cdot v
belongs to
W^{1,1}_{\mathrm{loc}}((0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{2})
and we have

(A.13)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \left\lVert\rho v\cdot u\right\rVert_{L^{1}_{t}L_{x}^{2}}&\leq\left\lVert\rho u\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{x}^{2}}\left\lVert\rho v\right\rVert_{L^{1}_{t}L_{x}^{\infty}}\\
\left\lVert\rho\nabla(v\cdot u)\right\rVert_{L^{1}_{t}L_{x}^{2}}&\leq\left\lVert\nabla v\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{t}L_{x}^{2}}\left\lVert\rho u\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{t}L_{x}^{\infty}}+\left\lVert\rho v\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{t}L_{x}^{\infty}}\left\lVert\nabla u\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{t}L_{x}^{2}}\\
\left\lVert\rho\partial_{t}(u\cdot v)\right\rVert_{L^{1}_{t}L^{1}_{x}}&\leq\left\lVert\rho\partial_{t}u\right\rVert_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\left\lVert\rho v\right\rVert_{L_{t}^{\infty}L^{2}_{x}}+\left\lVert\rho u\right\rVert_{L_{t}^{\infty}L^{2}_{x}}\left\lVert\rho\partial_{t}v\right\rVert_{L_{t}^{1}L^{2}_{x}}.\end{aligned}

By energy inequality
\rho u,\;\rho v\in L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}
and
\nabla u,\;\nabla v\in L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x}
. Away from initial time, by definitions of
Definition

1.3
and
Definition

2.1
we have

(A.14)

\displaystyle\rho\partial_{t}u,\;\rho\partial_{t}v\in L^{1}(({\varepsilon},\infty);L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})).

Fix
\varepsilon>0
and
T>0
.
We do not keep track of the precise dependence of the constants,
since it is not needed; all implicit constants may depend on
T
. By
Proposition

3.9
, for every
t\in[0,T]
,

(A.15)

\displaystyle\operatorname{supp}\rho(t)\subset D+B_{C\sqrt{t}}(0).

Applying
Corollary

3.6
with
v=v(t)
and
R=C
, we obtain

(A.16)

t^{\frac{1}{2}}\|v(t)\|_{L^{\infty}(D_{C,t})}\lesssim t^{\frac{3}{4}}\|\nabla v(t)\|_{L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\|\rho(t)\,v(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}.

Combining the support inclusion with (
A.16
), we deduce

(A.17)

\displaystyle t^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\rho(t)v(t)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

\displaystyle\lesssim t^{\frac{3}{4}}\|\nabla v(t)\|_{L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\|\rho(t)\,v(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

(A.18)

\displaystyle\lesssim t^{\frac{3}{4}}\|\nabla v(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla^{2}v(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{\frac{1}{2}}+\|\rho(t)\,v(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\lesssim_{\varepsilon}C_{(\rho,u)}(v).

In particular,

(A.19)

\displaystyle\rho v\in L^{\infty}_{\mathrm{loc}}\bigl((0,\infty);L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\bigr).

A similar computation shows that
\rho u\in L^{\infty}_{\mathrm{loc}}\bigl((0,\infty);L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\bigr)
. Hence we have proved that the right hand sides of (
A.13
) are bounded provided the time intervals are compact subsets of
(0,\infty)
.

We are left to study the continuity. First, we decompose

(A.20)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho({\varepsilon})u({\varepsilon})\cdot v({\varepsilon})-\rho_{0}u_{0}\cdot v_{0}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}[\rho({\varepsilon})u({\varepsilon})-\rho_{0}u_{0}]\cdot v_{0}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho({\varepsilon})u({\varepsilon})\cdot[v({\varepsilon})-v_{0}]

where the first addend converges to zero because
\mathbb{P}(\rho u)\in CL^{2}_{w}
, for the second addend we write

(A.21)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho u({\varepsilon})\cdot[v({\varepsilon})-v_{0})]

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\rho_{0}u_{0}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\left\lVert\rho({\varepsilon})(v({\varepsilon})-v_{0}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

(A.22)

\displaystyle\lesssim\underbrace{\left\lVert\rho({\varepsilon})v({\varepsilon})-\rho_{0}v_{0}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}}_{I({\varepsilon})}+\underbrace{\left\lVert(\rho({\varepsilon})-\rho_{0})v_{0}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}}_{J({\varepsilon})}.

I({\varepsilon})\to 0
because
\rho v\to\rho_{0}v_{0}
in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
by assumption. For the convergence of
J({\varepsilon})
let
\delta>0
and choose
M=M(\delta)
and
\tilde{{\varepsilon}}=\tilde{{\varepsilon}}(M,\delta)=\tilde{{\varepsilon}}(\delta)
such that

(A.23)

\displaystyle\left\lVert v_{0}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\left\lvert v_{0}\right\rvert>M)}<\delta,\qquad M\left\lVert\rho(\tilde{{\varepsilon}})-\rho_{0}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}<\delta.

The second choice is possible since
\rho\in C_{t}L^{2}_{x}
by
Corollary

3.8
. Then for
{\varepsilon}<\tilde{{\varepsilon}}
we have

(A.24)

\displaystyle J({\varepsilon})

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert(\rho({\varepsilon})-\rho_{0})v_{0}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\left\lvert v_{0}\right\rvert<M)}+\left\lVert(\rho({\varepsilon})-\rho_{0})v_{0}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\left\lvert v_{0}\right\rvert>M)}

(A.25)

\displaystyle\leq M\left\lVert\rho({\varepsilon})-\rho_{0}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\left\lVert\rho({\varepsilon})-\rho_{0}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2}))}\left\lVert v_{0}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\left\lvert v_{0}\right\rvert>M)}\leq 2\delta

which proves the convergence.
∎

Proposition A.4
.

Under the hypothesis of Scenario 1 we have for almost every
t>0

(A.26)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho(t)\,u(t)\cdot v(t)-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho_{0}\,u_{0}\cdot v_{0}

\displaystyle=-2\int_{0}^{t}\!\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\nabla u:\nabla v.

Proof.

By
Proposition

3.13
for almost every time in
t>0
we have, as
\left\lvert x\right\rvert\to\infty
,

(A.27)

\displaystyle\left\lvert u(t,x)\right\rvert=\left\lvert v(t,x)\right\rvert=O(1).

Then by
Corollary

3.14
with
v=u
and
h=v(t)
we get for almost every
t>0
that

(A.28)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho\dot{u}(t,x)\cdot v(t,x)\,\,\mathrm{d}x=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\nabla u(t,x):\nabla v(t,x)\,\,\mathrm{d}x.

and exchanging
u
and
v
in
Corollary

3.14
(using the shortcut
\dot{v}=D_{u}(v)
) we also obtain

(A.29)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho\dot{v}(t,x)\cdot u(t,x)\,\,\mathrm{d}x=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\nabla v(t,x):\nabla u(t,x)\,\,\mathrm{d}x.

Now we sum the two expressions and we integrate in
({\varepsilon},t)
with
{\varepsilon}>0

(A.30)

\displaystyle\int_{\varepsilon}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho\dot{u}\cdot v+\rho\dot{v}\cdot u=-2\int_{\varepsilon}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\nabla u:\nabla v

and we use
Corollary

A.3
, in particular (
A.11
), to say that

(A.31)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho(t)u(t)\cdot v(t)-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho({\varepsilon})u({\varepsilon})\cdot v({\varepsilon})=-2\int_{\varepsilon}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\nabla u:\nabla v.

The continuity for
{\varepsilon}\to 0
of the left-hand side is proved in
Corollary

A.3
and the convergence of the right-hand side is trivial since
\nabla u,\;\nabla v\in L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x}
.
∎

A.2.
The non linear case

We now focus on the following setting.

Scenario 2
.

(i)

(\rho_{1},u_{1})
and
(\rho_{2},u_{2})
are immediately strong solutions of (
1.2
) with initial data
(\rho_{0},u_{1}(0))
and
(\rho_{0},u_{2}(0))
, respectively, satisfying (

L
2

-patch
). Moreover,
v_{1}
and
v_{2}
are immediately strong solutions of (
2.1
), advected by
(\rho_{1},u_{1})
and
(\rho_{2},u_{2})
, respectively, with initial data
v_{1}(0)
and
v_{2}(0)
.

(ii)

We assume that
\rho_{2}v_{2}\to\rho_{0}v_{2}(0)
in
L^{2}_{x}
as
t\to 0
, and that

(A.32)

\displaystyle\sqrt{t}\left\lVert\nabla v_{2}(t)\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\to 0,\qquad\rho_{2}D_{u_{2}}(v_{2}),\;\sqrt{t}\,\nabla D_{u_{2}}(v_{2})\in L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x},\quad\nabla v_{2}\in L^{1}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x},

where all spaces are understood locally in time.

For notational convenience, we denote
\dot{v}_{i}:=D_{u_{i}}v_{i}
and
\dot{u}_{i}:=D_{u_{i}}u_{i}
, unless this causes ambiguity.

Theorem A.5
.

Under the assumptions of Scenario 2, for almost every
t>0
we have

(A.33)

\displaystyle\|(\rho_{1}\,\delta v)(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}

\displaystyle+2\int_{0}^{t}\|\nabla\delta v(s)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}\,ds\leq\|\rho_{0}\,\delta v(0)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}

(A.34)

\displaystyle\quad-2\int_{0}^{t}\!\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho_{1}\,\delta u\!\otimes\!\delta v:\nabla v_{2}-2\int_{0}^{t}\!\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\delta\rho\,\delta v\cdot\dot{v}_{2}.

Proof.

Using the energy inequality for both
(\rho_{1},v_{1})
and
(\rho_{2},v_{2})
, we obtain

(A.35)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \|(\rho_{1}\,\delta v)(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}&=\|(\rho_{1}v_{1})(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}-2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(\rho_{1}v_{1})(t)\cdot v_{2}(t)+\|(\rho_{1}v_{2})(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}\\
&=\|(\rho_{1}v_{1})(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}-2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(\rho_{1}v_{1})(t)\cdot v_{2}(t)+\|(\rho_{2}v_{2})(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\delta\rho(t)\,|v_{2}(t)|^{2}\\
&\leq\|\rho_{0}v_{1}(0)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}+\|\rho_{0}v_{2}(0)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}-2\int_{0}^{t}\Bigl(\|\nabla v_{1}(s)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}+\|\nabla v_{2}(s)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}\Bigr)\,ds\\
&\quad-2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(\rho_{1}v_{1})(t)\cdot v_{2}(t)+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\delta\rho(t)\,|v_{2}(t)|^{2}.\end{aligned}

For the last two terms in (
A.35
), we use
Proposition

A.7
. Combining (
A.35
) with (
A.63
), we obtain

(A.36)

\displaystyle\|(\rho_{1}\delta v)(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}\leq\,

\displaystyle\|\rho_{0}v_{1}(0)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}+\|\rho_{0}v_{2}(0)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}-2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho_{0}v_{1}(0)\cdot v_{2}(0)

(A.37)

\displaystyle-2\int_{0}^{t}\Bigl(\|\nabla v_{1}(s)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}+\|\nabla v_{2}(s)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}\Bigr)\,ds+4\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\nabla v_{1}:\nabla v_{2}

(A.38)

\displaystyle-2\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho_{1}\delta u\otimes\delta v:\nabla v_{2}-2\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\delta\rho\,\delta v\cdot\dot{v}_{2}.

By observing that

(A.39)

\displaystyle\|\nabla v_{1}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\nabla v_{2}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}-2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\nabla v_{1}:\nabla v_{2}=\|\nabla\delta v\|_{L^{2}}^{2},

we conclude the proof.
∎

To prove
Proposition

A.7
, we first need to establish regularity properties for cross quantities such as
\rho_{1}v_{2}
.

Lemma A.6
.

Under the assumptions of Scenario 2, we have

(A.40)

\displaystyle\delta\rho\,\dot{v}_{2}\in L_{\mathrm{loc}}^{1}([0,\infty);L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})),\qquad\delta\rho\,\delta v\in L_{\mathrm{loc}}^{\infty}([0,\infty);L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})).

Moreover, the following convergences hold:

(A.41)

\displaystyle\lim_{{\varepsilon}\to 0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(\rho_{1}v_{1})({\varepsilon})\cdot v_{2}({\varepsilon})=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho_{0}v_{1}(0)\cdot v_{2}(0)\qquad\lim_{{\varepsilon}\to 0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\delta\rho({\varepsilon})\,|v_{2}({\varepsilon})|^{2}=0.

Proof.

Fix
T>0
. Using
Proposition

3.9
for both
\rho_{1}
and
\rho_{2}
and picking the maximum among the two constants we get that for almost every
t\in(0,T)
,

(A.42)

\displaystyle\supp\rho_{i}(t)\subseteq D+B_{N\sqrt{t}}(0).

In particular, for every measurable function
v
,

(A.43)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\delta\rho(t)\,v\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq\left\lVert v\right\rVert_{L^{2}(D_{N,t})}.

Choosing
R=N
in
Corollary

3.6
, we obtain for both
i=1
and
i=2
, and for almost every
t\in(0,T)
,

(A.44)

\displaystyle\left\lVert v\right\rVert_{L^{2}(D_{N,t})}\lesssim\sqrt{t}\left\lVert\nabla v\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\left\lVert\rho_{i}(t)\,v\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}.

Choosing
v=\dot{v}_{2}(t)
and
i=2
, we infer

(A.45)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\delta\rho(t)\,\dot{v}_{2}(t)\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\lesssim\sqrt{t}\left\lVert\nabla\dot{v}_{2}(t)\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\left\lVert\rho_{2}(t)\,\dot{v}_{2}(t)\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})},

which proves that
\delta\rho\,\dot{v}_{2}\in L_{\mathrm{loc}}^{1}([0,\infty);L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2}))
. Choosing instead
v=v_{1}(t)
and
v=v_{2}(t)
, respectively, we obtain

(A.46)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \left\lVert\delta\rho(t)\,v_{1}(t)\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}&\lesssim\sqrt{t}\left\lVert\nabla v_{1}(t)\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\left\lVert\rho_{1}(t)\,v_{1}(t)\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})},\\
\left\lVert\delta\rho(t)\,v_{2}(t)\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}&\lesssim\sqrt{t}\left\lVert\nabla v_{2}(t)\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\left\lVert\rho_{2}(t)\,v_{2}(t)\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}.\end{aligned}

This proves that
\delta\rho\,\delta v\in L_{\mathrm{loc}}^{\infty}([0,\infty);L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2}))
.
The continuity at the initial time is nontrivial. We start by proving the second convergence in (
A.41
). Recall that
\delta\rho
solves

(A.47)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\delta\rho+\dive(\rho_{1}u_{1})=\dive(\rho_{2}u_{2}),\qquad\delta\rho(0)=0.

Testing (
A.47
) with a regular enough
\varphi
and integrating in time, we obtain for a.e.
t>0

(A.48)

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\delta\rho(t)\,\varphi\,dx\Big|

\displaystyle=\Big|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{D_{N,s}}\big(\rho_{1}u_{1}-\rho_{2}u_{2}\big)(s)\cdot\nabla\varphi\,dx\,ds\Big|

(A.49)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{t}\Big(\|u_{1}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}(D_{N,s})}+\|u_{2}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}(D_{N,s})}\Big)\|\nabla\varphi\|_{L^{1}(D_{N,s})}\,ds,

where we used (
A.42
) to restrict the domain of integration to
D_{N,s}
. By
Lemma

3.7
we have

(A.50)

\displaystyle\|u_{i}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}(D_{N,s})}\lesssim s^{-1/2},

and therefore

(A.51)

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\delta\rho(t)\,\varphi\,dx\Big|\lesssim\sqrt{t}\,\|\nabla\varphi\|_{L^{1}(D_{N,t})}.

Inserting
\varphi=|v_{2}(t)|^{2}
in (
A.51
), we obtain

(A.52)

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\delta\rho(t)\,|v_{2}(t)|^{2}\,dx\Big|\lesssim\|v_{2}(t)\|_{L^{2}(D_{N,t})}\big(\sqrt{t}\,\|\nabla v_{2}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\big).

By
Corollary

3.6
, the first factor is bounded in
L^{\infty}(0,t)
, and by assumption

(A.53)

\displaystyle\sqrt{t}\,\|\nabla v_{2}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\longrightarrow 0\qquad\text{as }t\downarrow 0.

Hence we conclude the second convergence in (
A.41
).

We now choose
\varphi=v_{2}(t)\cdot\psi
in (
A.51
), with
\psi\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
, and obtain

(A.54)

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\delta\rho(t)v_{2}(t)\cdot\psi\,dx\Big|\lesssim\|\psi\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\big(\sqrt{t}\,\|\nabla v_{2}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\big)+\sqrt{t}\,\|v_{2}(t)\|_{L^{2}(D_{N,t})}\,\|\nabla\psi\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})},

which converges to
0
as
t\to 0
. This proves that

(A.55)

\displaystyle\delta\rho(t)v_{2}(t)\rightharpoonup 0\quad\text{in }\dot{H}^{-1}(\mathbb{R}^{2}).

By (
A.46
) and a standard density argument we get

(A.56)

\displaystyle\delta\rho(t)v_{2}(t)\rightharpoonup 0\quad\text{in }L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2}).

The convergence of
\delta\rho\,|v_{2}|^{2}
at the initial time, combined with the fact that

\rho_{2}v_{2}\to\rho_{0}v_{2}(0)
in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
, also yields

(A.57)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho_{1}({\varepsilon})\,|v_{2}({\varepsilon})|^{2}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\delta\rho({\varepsilon})\,|v_{2}({\varepsilon})|^{2}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho_{2}({\varepsilon})\,|v_{2}({\varepsilon})|^{2}\longrightarrow\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho_{0}\,|v_{2}(0)|^{2}.

Moreover, for every
\varphi\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
we have

(A.58)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho_{1}({\varepsilon})v_{2}({\varepsilon})\cdot\varphi=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\delta\rho({\varepsilon})v_{2}({\varepsilon})\cdot\varphi+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho_{2}({\varepsilon})v_{2}({\varepsilon})\cdot\varphi\longrightarrow\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho_{0}v_{2}(0)\cdot\varphi.

Hence
(\rho_{1}v_{2})({\varepsilon})\to\rho_{0}v_{2}(0)
in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
.

We are now ready to prove the first convergence in (
A.41
). We write

(A.59)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(\rho_{1}v_{1})({\varepsilon})\cdot v_{2}({\varepsilon})-\rho_{0}\,v_{1}(0)\cdot v_{2}(0)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(\rho_{1}v_{1})({\varepsilon})\cdot\big[v_{2}({\varepsilon})-v_{2}(0)\big]

(A.60)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\big[(\rho_{1}v_{1})({\varepsilon})-\rho_{0}\,v_{1}(0)\big]\cdot v_{2}(0).

The second term converges to zero since
\mathbb{P}(\rho_{1}v_{1})\in C([0,T];L^{2}_{w})
and
v_{2}(0)
is divergence free. For the first term we estimate

(A.61)

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(\rho_{1}v_{1})({\varepsilon})\cdot\big[v_{2}({\varepsilon})-v_{2}(0)\big]\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\|\rho_{1}v_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\|\rho_{1}({\varepsilon})v_{2}({\varepsilon})-\rho_{1}({\varepsilon})v_{2}(0)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}

(A.62)

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\rho_{1}({\varepsilon})v_{2}({\varepsilon})-\rho_{0}v_{2}(0)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|(\rho_{1}-\rho_{0})v_{2}(0)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}.

The first term converges to zero by the strong convergence

(\rho_{1}v_{2})({\varepsilon})\to\rho_{0}v_{2}(0)
in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
, while the second one is handled exactly as
J(\varepsilon)
in
Corollary

A.3
.

∎

Proposition A.7
.

Under the assumptions of Scenario 2, for almost every
t>0
we have

(A.63)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} -\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho_{1}(t)\,v_{1}(t)&\cdot v_{2}(t)+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\delta\rho(t)\,|v_{2}(t)|^{2}=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho_{0}v_{1}(0)\cdot v_{2}(0)\\
&\quad-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho_{1}\,\delta u\otimes\delta v:\nabla v_{2}-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\delta\rho\,\delta v\cdot\dot{v}_{2}+2\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\nabla v_{1}:\nabla v_{2}.\end{aligned}

Proof.

We split the proof into three steps. In the first two steps, we prove two inequalities which, when combined in Step 3, yield the claim.

Step 1.
By
Proposition

3.13
, for almost every
t>0
and for
i=1,2
, we have
